That is what we have done, and we have reduced the pace considerably, significantly slowed down. We originally had – as in the last three years – an increase in the early retirement age by eight months per year. We slow down, even in the light of economic developments, to half, i.e. four months a year. That is, starting from now on, 2003, until the year 2017 this system of early pensions expires. I don't think anyone else can say that's a robbery. We have responded very substantially to the criticisms and suggestions of the social partners, and I hope that you will follow us along this path. Ladies and gentlemen! We have also considered that we are taking a 25-year transitional period in the area of throughput periods to be increased to 40 years. – This is not changed compared to the government template. We have planned not three but five years for the increase amounts. – This too is an area which significantly reduces the effects of this reform for the transition phase. It was very massive by the social partners – many of them were unfair and not comprehensible! – criticised that the losses for the individual insured can be very high in individual cases; up to 40, sometimes even up to 50 percent, was claimed. We have considered many of these examples – they were all wrong! We have put opposite examples on the table and have responded in addition, with a maximum loss limit of up to 10 percent. From all measures, whether to reduce the amount of increase, or to extend the calculation periods, or if you retire earlier, deductions cannot result in more loss than 10 percent. I believe that we have largely met the social partners' suggestions – for one thing I say to you, too: A reform that has no effect is not a reform either! – and this is in line with what Alfred Gusenbauer and others have said, namely that the overall objective is to reduce the pension level by about 10 percent compared to today’s situation. I believe that we can quite argue this maximum 10 percent – at the beginning it will be on average 3 percent for women and 5 percent for men. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] We have taken very seriously the objections, especially on the part of the workers, that the labour market may suffer massive tensions from early retirement measures. We held hours of discussions with experts, Professor Guger and Professor Marin, at the Round Table, and we also put out the figures. For the whole period, i.e. for the next fourteen years, an additional supply of 80 000 people on the labour market is added, i.e. about 5 000, 6 000 additional workers per year. We have responded by significantly slowing down the pace. We have responded by developing a generous reduction programme for additional wage costs – 2 billion schilling or 140 million € – so that the businesses can employ older workers for longer. We have introduced the right to qualification. We have the commitment of the employer side and the employee side that, in the event of a deterioration in the labour market situation, a special programme could be set in motion. We have extended the age part-time, the age transition allowance is significantly increased. – In total, a programme that sets out important accompanying measures for the labour market in particular, because we do not care if the argument is that while we take pension reform seriously, we leave the labour market issues in the background. Social is what creates work! – That must be our condition, that must be our objective, and we will do so, ladies and gentlemen. There is another problem: These pension funds have much more to do with gambling than with insurance. One only needs to look at the situation in the last three years since 1999 and at the development of exchange rates and thus the fund. Compared to someone who happened to retire in 1999, this would have made a difference of 40 percent for someone who is now in retirement in 2003, both would have paid the last 30 to 35 years according to the model you are promoting. This discount is due to the fact that you happen to retire just when the stock exchange is bad. And then you say that's a security measure! So I can only tell you: For the boys, this pension reform is certainly anything but a profit that pays for it. I think it could have been discussed – which you still reject – why we do not talk about a solidarity contribution in areas above the ASVG maximum pension, why we do not do more to those who are going to retire and have such high pensions. I would be interested in why the FPÖ is not involved in such a claim. We are of course particularly concerned that care services and also the gaps in the working life of women are taken into account. Without a better job for women in the pension reform, we would never have consented to her, and she would have been unthinkable for us, because of course it is the women who bring great benefits to the generation contract. I will not be tired of repeating once again the improvements to be made in this new pension reform, which we have heard in the whole debate today, especially by SPÖ and Greens. Pension-based child rearing periods are increased to two years, i.e. for women not only 15 years of work but 13 years of work to apply for a pension. And this applies retroactively from the beginning of the payment of child education. The extension of the calculation period was also balanced by taking into account three years per child. That is, if two children are there, six years will be counted. For example: If in 2010 the calculation period is 21 years, then it is 15 years for a woman with two children who are used as a calculation period. The basis for child rearing was also increased for pensions, so that in 2028 child rearing times are worth twice as much as they are now. We have also ensured improvements for women during the age period. The reduction in additional labour costs and the loss of unemployment insurance contributions will also give older workers better opportunities on the labour market in the future. These measures even make women better off than in the previous system. This, ladies and gentlemen, is free social policy! [[Congratulations with the liberals and representatives of the ÖVP.]] You, ladies and gentlemen of the SPÖ and the Greens, can't mess with everything, especially if you've never contributed to improvements yourself. Take note at last that the social state does not pay itself and that it is we who have assumed responsibility for the future of Austria!

The socially balanced pension model now provides that a total loss of 10 per cent can occur in each individual case. Many experts believe that this lid is too restricting the effectiveness of the reform, but I believe that the young generation is aware of its responsibility for our parents and grandparents, and that is why I say yes to this cover. This means that all horror reports from the trade union are from the table, and no one needs to be afraid of this reform. We of the Austrian People's Party are aware of our responsibility for the generations, and that is why important measures have been taken for the families. The pension-based proportion of child rearing periods – by the way, also an achievement of this government, which was fought most violently by the opposition – is currently increased from 18 months to 24 months. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] The calculation period is reduced by three years per child. In addition, the basis for pension-enhancing child rearing is increased to 150 percent in steps. Thus, all these measures help to support families, to honor child rearing times, to secure the yes to the child, the yes to our future. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] I can also tell you from a number of discussions with young people that they are aware of the responsibility for older workers, and therefore I welcome the measures for all those who can retire in the foreseeable future. The additional wage costs for older workers aged 56 and 58 are reduced to a level of 140 million. For unemployed workers over 50 years of age, the AMS will be entitled to training and reintegration measures. With regard to invalidity pension, the age of addition of currently 56 years is increased. For the years 2004 and 2005, the pension adjustment will be made by means of a fixed amount, whose calculation will be based on the value assurance objective rather than the net adjustment currently in force. I would also like to remind you of the tax exemption of annual income below €14 500. These measures mainly favour smaller incomes and pensions. The 80 percent cover is removed, and this means that the periods beyond 45 insurance years lead to higher pensions. Finally, I would like to say: While you, ladies and gentlemen of the union, with the support of SPÖ and Greens for purely party-political reasons, are insecure and driving away from their jobs on the road, we are taking responsibility from the government parties for this country and its people and have developed a social, fair and, above all, forward-looking pension model. It would really be a nice day for Austria if this concept of ÖVP and FPÖ could also be decided with the support of you, ladies and gentlemen of the opposition. I would like to invite you to this.
